The daily field guide for digital media

AI Media Scout

2026-11-10 · All platforms · Open-source software

Expanded open-source creative-AI research across 96 fields, 815 repository queries and 23 model tasks, supplemented by live primary sources beyond GitHub. This edition provides 28 complete guides and 161 additional eligible screened discoveries; four guides complete earlier pending entries. It includes local media/voice workflows, agent-driven creative tools, fabrication experiments, photobook layout and learned print watermarking. No discovered application was installed or run. New entries remain Quality unverified or explicitly Experimental where current prototype evidence supports that label. A separate, scoped OIDN library assessment supports Ready to try for CPU single-frame denoising, with results still documented and excluded workflows stated. All 2,215 previously published tools already had current-week reviews; new entries receive today’s six-check assessment. Raw unreviewed, duplicate, non-AI and unresolved-license candidates stay local or in the watchlist rather than becoming library entries. The collector was interrupted after all 815 search queries and 23 model-task responses were captured. Recovery reuses those same-day search captures and completes repository reads; it is not a second fresh search. Original-run network totals were not recoverable, so recovery counters are not whole-run totals. No whole-Internet or exhaustive search claim is made. Verified collection: 815/815 repository queries attempted; 815 successful, 0 partial, 0 failed and 106 bounded/truncated. All 23 model tasks were attempted; 0 failed. These are bounded discovery attempts, not an exhaustive census or quality approval. Separate from successful search queries, 43 watchlist fetches and 4 enrichment attempts failed; the detailed warnings remain in the report. A 404/409 is an availability gap, not proof of abandonment or license revocation.

Search and review counts

96 creative fields; 815/815 repository queries attempted; 23/23 model-task queries attempted. 38553 distinct source candidates and 970 model leads. 28 detailed profiles and 161 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 0 partial repository queries, 106 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 0 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

Profiles are based on current primary documentation. Creative quality and performance claims are attributed to the developers unless hands-on testing is explicitly recorded. Unknown hardware requirements stay unknown. Code, model weights, hosted services, and required proprietary hosts can have different terms.

openedit (afsh4ck)

Documented creative workflow; quality confidence assessed separately · MIT · recently-created

The repository was created on 2026-10-15. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

Docs checked 2026-11-10 · Documentation review; installation not tested

Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Original software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. Independent quality and full reproduction remain unverified.

Scope: Prepare reviewed transcript-based cuts, animated overlays, captions and a local multi-track edit from owned footage.

How it uses AI

A learned encoder/decoder hides a short message in a photograph and recovers it after printing and camera capture; the repository supplies training, image encoding/decoding and detector code. [1] [3]

Introduction

A learned encoder/decoder hides a short message in a photograph and recovers it after printing and camera capture; the repository supplies training, image encoding/decoding and detector code. [1] [3]

Artist-book, exhibition-photograph and printed-image experiments that connect a physical image to a small machine-readable message. [1] [3]

How it uses AI

Lightroom Classic plugin that uses local RF-DETR segmentation and optional BiRefNet refinement, or exports Lightroom-generated AI masks. [1] [3]

Introduction

Use a Mac with Nix flakes enabled and a separately signed-in coding-agent CLI. Obtain the documented Whisper.cpp large-v3-turbo model (about 1.6 GB), clone outside the default ~/Studio library, and use the pinned Nix install path or ./studio ui. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. For source instructions, open a terminal in the checked-out project folder and choose the documented route for your platform. Release installers follow their own steps.
  3. Use a Mac with Nix flakes enabled and a separately signed-in coding-agent CLI. Obtain the documented Whisper.cpp large-v3-turbo model (about 1.6 GB), clone outside the default ~/Studio library, and use the pinned Nix install path or ./studio ui.
nix run .#install
./studio ui

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Import a small footage folder into a separate studio library.
  2. Transcribe and inspect source words before asking the selected agent to find moments.
  3. Review cut boundaries, captions, framing and post drafts in the shared project.
  4. Export and inspect the actual video; approval, scheduling and distribution are not implemented.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
The documented speech model is approximately 1.6 GB. No minimum RAM/VRAM, supported Mac generation or total footage/cache disk budget is documented.
Software
Mac workflow, Nix with flakes and its pinned Python/FFmpeg/Whisper.cpp environment; separate signed-in Claude Code, Codex, Goose or compatible command-line agent for moment selection and drafts.
Platforms
macOS documented; Windows and Linux native acceptance not established

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: Studio code is MIT. Whisper weights, Nix-resolved tools and the selected agent service retain separate terms. Agent inference can require a network and paid plan; local transcription/editing does not make cloud-agent prompts local.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. Studio code is MIT. Whisper weights, Nix-resolved tools and the selected agent service retain separate terms. Agent inference can require a network and paid plan; local transcription/editing does not make cloud-agent prompts local.
  • Cost: Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

Limitations

No face-aware reframing, SDR BT.709 export with HDR tone mapping, per-file Whisper loading and project-specific transcript fixes. Preview does not show every pace/voice/cover effect exactly as export. Mac app is ad hoc signed, not notarized; identity, approving, scheduling and publishing posts remain future work. [1]

Hardware & software

Software
Python 3.10+, qwen-tts for normal modes. Fast modes use a separate environment with the documented torch 2.11 cu128, transformers 5.15.1 and an unmerged batched fork. Optional CosyVoice/CAM++, FFmpeg and vgmstream have separate setup.
Platforms
Windows 10/11 is the documented workflow. The server is cross-platform Python, but the paths and hotkey workflow are Windows-first; complete macOS/Linux compatibility is not established.

Iterating a documentary or travel-film edit with an agent while keeping original footage separate and collecting comments on each cut. [1] [3]

Demo & examples

The author describes seven cuts of one real travel film on Linux and supplies a synthetic demo. This is author use, not independent validation; Apple Silicon and WSL2 routes remain untested on real hardware in this source. [1] [3]

Install

Install Docker and Git for the documented host, obtain cutter, then use cutter doctor and the agent setup command or CLI. The launcher pulls/builds the container on first use; FilmCraft and Python dependencies are in the image. An AI client and its model access are separate. [1] [3]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. For source instructions, open a terminal in the checked-out project folder and choose the documented route for your platform. Release installers follow their own steps.
  3. Install Docker and Git for the documented host, obtain cutter, then use cutter doctor and the agent setup command or CLI. The launcher pulls/builds the container on first use; FilmCraft and Python dependencies are in the image. An AI client and its model access are separate.
git clone https://github.com/aaronblankenburg/cutter
cd cutter
./bin/cutter doctor

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3]

  1. Create a new project pointing to a short folder of source footage; give the agent a target length, style and music-rights constraint.
  2. Analyze footage, inspect transcript/contact sheets, then have the agent write and review the edit plan.
  3. Build/export one cut, inspect it, and collect timecoded feedback before another revision. A fresh clone’s bin/cutter must be invoked directly or placed on PATH before bare cutter commands work.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3]

Hardware
No GPU is required for the documented CPU route; Linux NVIDIA acceleration is optional. The image is about 1.8 GB installed/0.7 GB download and Whisper about 1.6 GB, plus project/export storage. RAM and VRAM minima are not published.
Software
Docker, Git and a compatible agent (author tested Claude Code/Codex). The image carries FilmCraft, FFmpeg and pinned Python 3.12 dependencies. Reviewers need a browser.
Platforms
Linux x86_64 was used for one film. Apple Silicon macOS support is documented but not yet hardware-tested by the author; Windows requires WSL2/Docker and is untested.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1] [3]

  • Weights: Original code is MIT; the container includes FilmCraft MIT/Apache, FFmpeg GPL and separate Python dependencies. Whisper weights, source footage and music have separate terms. Agent provider processing/charges are separate from local transcription.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. Original code is MIT; the container includes FilmCraft MIT/Apache, FFmpeg GPL and separate Python dependencies. Whisper weights, source footage and music have separate terms. Agent provider processing/charges are separate from local transcription.
  • Cost: Local compute/storage, any chosen AI agent/model service, and properly licensed media/music.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1] [3]

Limitations

A local render does not mean agent prompts/contact sheets remain on-device. Author CPU rendering is slow for long projects, manual FilmCraft edits must be communicated to the agent, and the default review server uses plain HTTP for a trusted local network. No independent deployment/security review is claimed. [1] [3]

How it uses AI

An agent-oriented video-analysis CLI combining shot/audio measurements with optional vision-model and agent review to prepare evidence-linked remake plans. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Introduction

An agent-oriented video-analysis CLI combining shot/audio measurements with optional vision-model and agent review to prepare evidence-linked remake plans. [1] [3] [4] [5]

What it is good for

Learn a reference clip’s cuts, camera/lighting setups, motion graphics and audio structure, then plan your own production using separately licensed assets. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Demo & examples

The README publishes per-shot author reports, frame examples and evaluation tables. Re-encoded examples can produce different results; author benchmarks are not independent proof of forensic AI detection accuracy. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Install

Install Python 3.10+ and FFmpeg, clone the official repository, then follow its documented install_skill.sh route to install the CLI and link the workflow into a compatible agent client. Confirm rea-video --help before using your own short clip; the linked CLI guide describes the actual commands. [1] [3] [4] [5]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. For source instructions, open a terminal in the checked-out project folder and choose the documented route for your platform. Release installers follow their own steps.
  3. Install Python 3.10+ and FFmpeg, clone the official repository, then follow its documented install_skill.sh route to install the CLI and link the workflow into a compatible agent client. Confirm rea-video --help before using your own short clip; the linked CLI guide describes the actual commands.
git clone https://github.com/JuneYaooo/rea-video
cd rea-video
bash scripts/install_skill.sh
rea-video --help

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3] [4] [5]

  1. Provide a short clip you have permission to analyze to a compatible agent workflow.
  2. Inspect extracted shot frames, measurements and conflicts before accepting filmed/AI/code judgments.
  3. Review report.html and its proposed filming, generation and coding tasks.
  4. Treat any remake as a separate production; this package itself analyzes and plans rather than rendering a new video.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Python 3.10+, FFmpeg and a command-capable agent for the reviewed AI-assisted workflow. Optional Gemini/Doubao-compatible video endpoints add separate network, account and cost requirements.
Platforms
macOS Homebrew and Linux apt prerequisite examples documented; Windows native setup/acceptance is not established in the reviewed instructions

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1] [3] [4] [5]

  • Weights: Apache-2.0 code with a retained project NOTICE. Vision/agent services, optional capsule-cinema generation, source footage and output assets have separate terms; no rights transfer follows from analysis.
  • Commercial: Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 code with a retained project NOTICE. Vision/agent services, optional capsule-cinema generation, source footage and output assets have separate terms; no rights transfer follows from analysis.
  • Cost: Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

audio.cpp

Documented creative workflow; quality confidence assessed separately · Apache-2.0 · first-profile

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

Docs checked 2026-11-10 · 4,149 GitHub stars · Release v0.9.4 (2026-11-05) · Documentation review; installation not tested

Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Prototype local spoken narration or speech-recognition pipelines with explicit model-family/backend selection rather than relying on a single cloud audio service.

Evidence checked 2026-11-10; documentation review does not imply hands-on testing.

  • Open-source software license · verified: The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [2]
  • Creative results · documented: The README supplies PocketTTS synthesis and server/UI examples plus model-specific guides. A broad support table does not establish every model/backend combination or audio quality. [1]
  • Reproducible setup · documented: Choose a matching official Windows, Ubuntu or macOS binary, or use the documented compiler/backend build route. Obtain the selected model through the model manager and read that family’s setup instructions. Windows CUDA builds need their companion runtime archive beside the binaries. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [1]
  • Maintenance and support · unknown: A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
  • Independent use · unknown: Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
  • Models, services and costs · documented: Complete root code grant is Apache-2.0, including standard Sections 1–9 and the project copyright application notice. Weights/conversions and optional runtime components have separate terms; a GGUF file is not inherently compatible or commercially cleared. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [1]

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Backend/model coverage is explicitly model-dependent; arbitrary llama.cpp/whisper.cpp GGUF files are not automatically supported. HIP and Apple XCFramework packaging have unresolved validation limits. RAM/VRAM/storage requirements must be taken from the selected model, not inferred from the C++ implementation.

How it uses AI

Pure C++/ggml inference toolkit with CLI, server and embedded WebUI for documented speech synthesis, cloning, recognition and other model-specific audio tasks. [1]

Introduction

Pure C++/ggml inference toolkit with CLI, server and embedded WebUI for documented speech synthesis, cloning, recognition and other model-specific audio tasks. [1]

What it is good for

Prototype local spoken narration or speech-recognition pipelines with explicit model-family/backend selection rather than relying on a single cloud audio service. [1]

Demo & examples

The README supplies PocketTTS synthesis and server/UI examples plus model-specific guides. A broad support table does not establish every model/backend combination or audio quality. [1]

Install

Choose a matching official Windows, Ubuntu or macOS binary, or use the documented compiler/backend build route. Obtain the selected model through the model manager and read that family’s setup instructions. Windows CUDA builds need their companion runtime archive beside the binaries. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. For source instructions, open a terminal in the checked-out project folder and choose the documented route for your platform. Release installers follow their own steps.
  3. Choose a matching official Windows, Ubuntu or macOS binary, or use the documented compiler/backend build route. Obtain the selected model through the model manager and read that family’s setup instructions. Windows CUDA builds need their companion runtime archive beside the binaries.

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Choose one documented model family and independently check its weight terms and model-specific requirements.
  2. Use the embedded local UI or CLI with explicit --family, --task, --model and backend selectors.
  3. Generate one short narration or transcribe a short authorized recording, then inspect output and timing.
  4. Only add streaming, conversion or multi-stage pipelines after verifying support for that exact family/backend.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Prebuilt inference and embedded UI do not require Python, but some model downloads/conversions invoke the Python model manager. Source: Linux GCC 13+, Windows Visual Studio Build Tools 2022+ with C++/CMake/Ninja, or Mac Xcode tools/CMake and the Metal compiler. GPU toolchains are backend-specific.
Platforms
Windows x64 CPU/Vulkan/CUDA; HIP packages are test builds; Ubuntu x64 CPU/Vulkan; HIP packages are test builds; macOS arm64/x64 Metal packages documented

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: Complete root code grant is Apache-2.0, including standard Sections 1–9 and the project copyright application notice. Weights/conversions and optional runtime components have separate terms; a GGUF file is not inherently compatible or commercially cleared.
  • Commercial: Software use follows Apache-2.0. Complete root code grant is Apache-2.0, including standard Sections 1–9 and the project copyright application notice. Weights/conversions and optional runtime components have separate terms; a GGUF file is not inherently compatible or commercially cleared.
  • Cost: Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

HIP and Apple XCFramework packaging have unresolved validation limits. RAM/VRAM/storage requirements must be taken from the selected model, not inferred from the C++ implementation. [1]

Excluded and unresolved findings

These findings are retained as research notes and are not part of the eligible tool library.

  • Overstar66/prompt-workbench · needs-license-review: The full root MIT grant was read, but the README adds personal-learning/creation-only wording. Whether that wording restricts the software or only the included assets/workflow remains unresolved; hold pending clarification. Source Source
  • light-cloud-templates/ai-social-media-post-generator · excluded: A fresh repository read returned HTTP 404 after the initial same-day MIT/source capture. Its current download and setup cannot be established. The earlier capture is retained locally; 404 alone does not prove abandonment or license revocation. Source Source
  • TEN-framework/ten-framework · excluded: The complete current grant adds device-deployment, competing-service and application-benefit restrictions to an Apache notice. This is not an unrestricted Apache-2.0 software grant. Source Source
  • ddean2009/MoneyPrinterPlus · excluded: The complete GPL file appends a personal/educational-use-only grant and prohibits commercial use without permission. The whole current software grant fails the open-source-only scope. Source Source
  • ml5js/ml5-library · needs-license-review: The current complete grant adds a Code-of-Conduct condition and a time-based reversion. An eventual MIT grant does not establish an unmodified current MIT grant. Source Source
  • nexu-io/open-design · needs-license-review: The full root grant differs substantively from standard Apache-2.0, including the trademark clause and the section on liability/support obligations. A recognized SPDX badge is insufficient to clear the whole current grant. Source Source
  • EasyDiffusion/easydiffusion · excluded: Current full software terms include use-based restrictions. The familiar diffusion workflow does not exempt the code from this project’s open-source-only policy. Source Source
  • hypit-ai/hypit · excluded: The complete current grant adds commercial/multitenant restrictions. Public source and a familiar base-license name do not establish unrestricted open-source software. Source Source
  • Saul-Ibanez-Garcia/riluma-intelligent-studio · excluded: The complete GPL grant was read, but the current source explicitly supplies a foundation while editor/media/AI functionality remains unimplemented. No usable creative AI tool was established. Source Source
  • ali-vilab/dreamtalk · needs-license-review: The root MIT text conflicts with explicit research/non-commercial wording in the README. The complete workflow grant needs clarification. Source Source
  • FACEGOOD/FACEGOOD-Audio2Face · needs-license-review: Bundled test/Unreal-character non-commercial terms leave workflow-wide code/asset scope unresolved; the root grant alone does not settle all included material. Source Source
  • jayin92/Skyfall-GS · needs-license-review: The additional Inria renderer license requires a separate scope review; root licensing alone does not establish the full rendering path as eligible. Source Source Source
  • Xotion Studio · excluded: The complete current software grant is PolyForm Noncommercial, so this GitLab motion-video project is excluded from the open-source-only library. Source
